ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u1	Good morning. The session is open. The agenda is published in the Official Gazette of Parliament, so we do not proceed to its reading. He justified the inattentiveness of Raúl Fernández Fernández, with a health problem. We wish him his prompt recovery. And before we get on the agenda, we also tell all Members of Parliament and all the workers in Parliament who started the influenza vaccination campaign and the medical services are at their disposal to make use of vaccination. Preventive measures, it would be good for everyone. Let's start with the legislative text. Comprehensive discussion of the draft law on public shows and recreational activities
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u2	This bill has been amended to the full Parliamentary Group on Return, document 18398. Mr. Alfonso Rueda Valenzuela has the floor for the introduction of the bill. Whenever you want.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u3	Thank you, Mr. President. Good morning, ladies. It brings Galician Union to this plenary the Bill of Public Shows and Recreational Activities of Galicia, which is the first law in our Autonomous Community to establish a generic, up-to-date and global regulation in an area where it has not until now, at least one autonomous regulation. The first thing I have to say, ladies and gentlemen, is that this text aims to be from the very beginning the result of a broad consensus and a broad consultation process. Of course, the intention was to provide Galicia with a general regulation that aims to cover from the great events that are held in Galicia, the great public shows, to the multitude of popular verbenas that also take place, and in the midst of many public shows of different fluency and different repercussions, but which, in any case — and as you all know — have a very important impact in Galicia. Of course, this would be impossible without the participation of all those who will be affected in one way or another. So we try to reconcile interests that were often countered, to meet the demands of the sector and also to meet the demands of citizenship and of the users and recipients of all these shows. I believe that at least the consultation process — and I believe that there is a high level of consensus — has been an objective that has been achieved. So you can make an idea, you've spoken to a lot of people. To highlight some: the Federation of Businesses of Conference Rooms, Dance and Discotheques of Galicia; the Confederation of Businesses of Hostalerie of Galicia; the Galaga Orchestras Association; the Association of Businesses of Verbena Galaga; the Galaga Association of Musical Companies; associations of neighbors; of course, the Galician Federation of Municipalities and Provinces. Some suggestions from many management centres with sectoral competences affected in one way or another by this bill were found in the Autonomic Administration. Finally, we try to collect as many sensitivity as possible, as many suggestions as possible. And, of course, this draft was subject to public exposure at the Transparency Portal. It's just as much as saying that all the citizenship of Galicia had the opportunity to participate in their approaches or suggestions. A total of 67 allegations were received. We believe that for a new creation regulation that affects a considerable number of people and groups there are not many allegations. So it means that, in principle, the text, perhaps for that task of consensus and prior consultation, already had a degree of acceptance. I must therefore also thank those parliamentary groups, on behalf of the Government of Greece, who have decided, regardless of the approaches and amendments that may be made during the processing procedure, not to present amendments to the whole, which were all the groups but one. By entering into the content of the project, as they were progressing, we wanted to give you a double perspective: paying attention to the general interest of citizenship — at the end many people in one way or another will be passive recipients or subjects of the provisions of this law — and also trying to reconcile the different interests of many professional sectors to those who will affect the entry into force of this law. He's got a lot of news. By marking those that have a clearer impact, I'd like to stop at three. First. The law seeks, seeks, and I believe it reaches the protection of children and young people. An explicit reference is made to the obligations already introduced in the current legislation on the prevention of the consumption of alcoholic beverages in minors and also complements and extends the restrictions already provided for in other regulations on access to public shows and recreational activities of minors. Secondly, there is a pretension to seeking the maximum security and defence of people who attend public shows. We collect in this text the rights and obligations of the people who attend these events, but also of the artists, also obligations above all of the organizers, and also rights, and we provide for a sanctioning regime, which is directed above all — not exclusively, but especially — to ensure security during public shows and also security around the venues where these shows are held. With regard to safety, I would particularly like to highlight the consideration as a very serious infringement of the fact that it exceeds the maximum public capacity, and in addition to this accentuated gravity and also in sanctions when this entails a serious risk to the security of goods and, above all, of people. We have established sanctions that can reach up to EUR 600,000 and, of course, the prohibition — which can be definitive — to never carry out such activities again for the organizers who commit these infringements. We also regulate the access control service in this line. It is mandatory to have self-qualified personnel in accordance with autonomous regulations and also to regulate their own monitoring and security services in certain types of shows. We also collect the assumptions that these activities have an obligation to have a prior emergency and self-protection plan. And the security requirements for event organizers go beyond that. Of course, you have to have complaint sheets for the employment of the people who want it. Minimum information data to be provided and the organizers' publicity for the events they promote are set. For example, in relation to the latter aspect, the percentage of entries that are put on sale to the public through the distribution establishments should be explained; if it has not been established, it is understood that they have a minimum of 60% of the localities available for sale; and, of course, the resale of entries is also prohibited and sanctioned. The third axis of the three I referred to before is protection against discrimination when it comes to access to public shows and also protection of animals and protection of the environment. The law establishes, for example, the prohibition of any kind of public spectacle or recreational activity that incites or may promote discrimination on the basis of race, gender identity, sexual orientation, religion, opinion, disability or any other condition or personal or social circumstances, and establishes the corresponding penalties for such infringements. All events that violate animal protection regulations or those that cannot guarantee the compensation of property, especially those that are under cataloguing included in Galicia's cultural or natural heritage, are equally prohibited. And there are also special precautions for the right of admission, to prevent it from happening what it used to say: any kind of discrimination against persons who may limit or even prevent the right of access to public shows and recreational activities. Another novelty that will prevent the existence of first or second audiences is the general ban, as I said before, on the resale of tickets, including the online resale. This prohibition is set out in Article 18 of this bill. By stopping a little more in the news that reaches the show sector more directly and recreational activities, as well as the responsibilities of the municipalities and the autonomous administration itself, which are also established, I would like to highlight the following. First. The administrative intervention regime is the attention to the particularities of the territory where the public spectacle is to be held. A responsible statement is established as the main administrative intervention regime. We have amended Law 9/2013 of 19 December of Galicia's economic entrepreneurship and competitiveness to simplify the processing of permits in respect of shows; in addition, this is in line with what European legislation prescribes. And, in addition, given the very particular and special impact that everything in Galicia has to do with the celebration of verbenas and popular parties that organize groups of neighbors, party committees, they already clarify in a special way — I believe that they are perfectly clarified — the procedures that must be followed in these cases, collecting in addition to the sensitivity and many suggestions that have been made and known on these last few dates about the need for the law to be perfectly delineated everything that has to do with the requirements of celebrations and popular verbenas. Therefore, neighborhood groups and party committees will simply have to submit a responsible statement, together with the commitment to hire an insurance — this is provided for in the regulations — or accreditation documentation that this insurance is available for the organization of these events. This would be the general practice — I say — very simplified, and I think with minimum guarantees that are the desirable ones and those that demand the law, and that, when it comes to activities that include installation assemblies subject to the declaration responsible for the licence, it will have to process it to the person who is the holder of the installation, i.e. the employer, and it is not made that burden on the party commissions or neighboring groups. As far as the municipalities are concerned, it must be said that they are now performing new functions in the field of competition in public shows. The possibility of being the municipalities themselves, without needing to seek authorization, such as it has until now been passed to the Autonomic Administration, is introduced as a novelty, those who decide, within certain requirements which will be laid down by regulation, extensions or reductions on the schedule of activities according to special circumstances and certain periods of the year. As you know, the general schedule is marked from the Galician Union in a June 2005 order and what will be done, once the law enters into force, is regularly amended also, according to all the groups concerned, the timetables for establishing general schedules and then — I insist — will give the municipalities the ability, without requiring each time to seek authorization from the Autonomous Community, to extend or reduce these general schedules. The preliminary draft also includes the possibility that the municipalities may authorize extraordinary activities, as well as the obligation to approve an inspection plan for shows, activities or establishments open to the public to which the municipal licence is given or to which the responsible declaration is given. What we are trying to do here, ladies and gentlemen, is to involve in the fulfilment of the conditions set out in the submitted documentation and in the responsible statement. This results in greater security and a planned inspection plan that should not lead to greater problems. Third and last. Also — as I said — obligations are laid down for the autonomous administration. It must be the administration of Galician board, the autonomous administration, which will drive the creation — and we will of course do so — of the Committee on Public Shows and Recreational Activities of Galicia — as provided for in Article 10 of the rule. We will, of course, invite Fegamp, invite people from the different sectors affected. It will be an advisory body, but it will also make proposals and follow-up, especially in the early stages of the application of the law, of all its provisions, so that this initial consensus with which it is born also presides over all its application, and also the changes and proposed amendments that were necessary. And last of all, it's going to be the Galician board that's responsible for creating and carrying out a register of companies and sector establishments, which we also consider very necessary. Logically, we will lack the collaboration of the municipalities through the documentation they receive from responsible statements, especially to go and create this record, keep it and know exactly who it is doing and what it is doing in the field of public shows at every moment in Galicia and in every one of its municipalities. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, these are the most relevant news of a bill that, I insist, has been subject to many consultations, has gathered a lot of suggestions and what it is to have a vocation — it is true — in a sector with so many cases. Then there'll have to be a regulation, a regulatory development, but it's also necessary a general regulation with a range of laws, and that's what we want with this project. You know that Galicia has exclusive competence in the field of public shows, a transfer made in 1996, and so this may make the need for this regulation even more evident and necessary. It was time to establish its own legislation that adapted to the peculiarities that Galicia has and adapted to the peculiarities above all of certain celebrations that have a special, special, as I said before, incidence in Galicia and some peculiarities that do not occur in other territories of the State. So we try to take into account the diversity of situations and the need to guarantee the rights of people, the assistant public, their fundamental security and also clearly mark the obligations of public show organizers. Therefore, I am only grateful for the contributions made — I insist — by many sectors, by many groups, by many groups, for all those that can be done in the process of amendments if, as the Government hopes, it goes beyond this initial phase, and among all of us having a law that — I say — was time to have it and that is born with a vocation of regulatory development. It will be necessary — as soon as possible here, the law itself says in its explanatory statement, but also in certain general aspects with continuity and vocation to regulate an activity that in Galicia — they know above all those who are dedicated to it — has a great impact, has a lot of economic impact and has a lot of social impact. So it was a duty to regulate it, and that's what we're doing now. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u4	Thank you, Vice President. The defence of the amendment to the full refund. Parliamentary Group of En Marea, Mrs Cuña.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u5	Thank you, Mr. President. Good morning to all and to all. Counsellor, Mr. Alfonso Rueda, we agreed on a starting point: in need; there was already time of course; the need is unquestionable, the need to regulate such a wide and complex sector. This law, this project, we believe it must be returned, because we have found many defects difficult to remedy. We believe, moreover, that partial amendments will not remedy the waves we detect, because either they will be cosmetics or they will either leave the legal text inefficient, with consequent delay with respect to other communities. This law has been developed in virtually all autonomous communities of the state. If a more silent law is to be sought, the restructuring must of course be enormous. Let's explain why. We consider it unrealistic. It's true that it's a vast sector, it's true that regulation is very complex, but it's not done with feet on the ground from our point of view. And we'll explain it for the kid. There is little development, and there is certainly a noticeable and worrying loss in the participation of citizenship. You speak of consensus, we say that it has not been properly agreed. He listed with which groups, with which sectors they communicated, but we know that there are many affected sectors that were neither called nor heard nor, of course, established any kind of dialogue. To set an example, we have to talk about the Consumers' Association. It's important that the Consumer Association is part of a law that affects a lot of people. You haven't talked to a lot of promoters and agents involved. We haven't talked to any people in the so-called creative industry, since then with no association of cultural industries, nor with many other associations in the show sectors. Obviously they lack points of view and look good. How can one develop, with minimal encounters, minimal technical and political meetings, a debate that has been pending for decades, as you said? Leisure has changed a lot, very much. It is essential to legislate, to create a regulatory framework consistent with the current reality and taking into account all those involved. The leisure sector has grown a lot, but, Mr. Rueda, not all leisure is business, and this is important. You only speak and in the law you only talk about mercantilist aspects. It's true that part has enormous economic importance; 2% of large companies, large orchestras have a lot of economic importance; they've certainly had an agreement for very little time. But there is another part that performs other kinds of activities, artistic activities of social transformation, of free and free expression at times, which cannot be recorded with administrative procedures or strong economic sanctions; all of them must be present and have voice as well. In a situation of exhausting crisis that left a desolate, devastated landscape in the economic, social and cultural structure, the Act of Shows and Activities should have a relevant updateer role. We don't think he's got it. There is no recovery, no increase, special attention to companies with social responsibility, for example. Another question: there is a key that is the balance, the balance between security, which is necessary; the balance between freedom, which is essential; and the balance between coexistence. When we talk about these three balances, we talk about freedom of thought, we talk about freedom of creation, we talk about freedom of expression, and we have to take very seriously that these balances are produced; it seems that the balance is unbalanced. There's a lot of the law that's very detailed, especially when it comes to the sanctioning issue, but there's a part of the law that's outlined and not developed at all. So we believe that citizenship is free, free to choose its form of leisure and fun, and the promoters of shows to make a wide offer, without any limit to the legal obligations, of course, derived from general interest, for reasons of security, coexistence and respect for people's rights. It's a law, Counselor, very abstract. It aims to match, put in the same box shows like a monologue, a musical performance, with big events of a complex scale. This premise is wrong: to attempt to equate non-profit activities, only recreational activities or simply social dynamisation that play a key role in the socialization of communities. The rule should be very careful to differentiate what kind of activity we're dealing with. It must be said that the law, at the beginning, at the beginning, has no definition, there is a lack of definition. It is not clearly defined that it is a public spectacle, which is an occasional, extraordinary spectacle, fixed or possible recreational activities, public institutions, facilities, open spaces, owners of the establishment, organizers, artists, performers, performers... there is a problem with exclusion and inclusion, with whom it should be, it is not valid. When everything's worth, it's not worth anything, and they're not worth the same demands for a big event as for a small performance. The definition. By setting an example, it includes non-profit artistic and cultural activities in the public show category. This is disproportionate and it injures rights. The law also does not allow free and free expression, the law does not exclude acts organized by civic groups and endangers small groups and intermediate spaces. Intermediate spaces are needed so that many people who want to approach or dedicate themselves to culture in their multiple variants can take their first steps or get close to places where other events do not arrive. Balance or avoid the gap between rural and urban, eliminate those cracks, it should be. It's incredible and almost horrifying that these kinds of activities are included in the category of public shows, as you can seriously confuse their nature and artistic and cultural condition of creation with free knowledge. On the other hand, there are no other kinds of issues, such as not taking into account something that is fundamental to us, the law is very restrictive. When we were talking about the balance between security, freedom and coexistence, we believed that this law could collect very good parameters that have to do with principles. When we make amendments to the whole, we refer clearly and above all to the philosophy of the law — the other things can be changed on time — but we detect an amalgam of inadequacies, an absolute necessity of a law that really values the differences in our community with respect to others and gives satisfaction to the problems that are currently being posed and that principles that really deal with the evolution of our society are accepted. We are talking, for example, about the balance between these three principles and that freedom of creation, thought and expression is contemplated, not only security, but also others. We believe that in the beginning of the law it must be essential to provide citizenship with a leading role, ensuring that regulation does not restrict freedoms, to facilitate people holding and organising shows and recreational activities, so that only those who objectively require them are subject to license or authorization. You were also talking about counsel or commission. The council and commission are undeveloped, you don't know who's going to be part of it; you just talk about considering Fegamp, municipalities and municipalities, but the regulatory link shouldn't numb essential freedoms, and we believe they're also tools that we need in the right to access. He spoke of adapting to European legislation. Yes, it is true that it is adapted to European legislation, especially as it relates — and cannot be otherwise — to the commercial issue, but it must be said that everything is not commodity, it must be said that not all is speculation, leisure and business, not all must be subject to market dynamics; artistic and cultural expression has another dimension and must be contemplated. When talking at European level about minimal administrative intervention and simplification, the new law should establish a general system of communication in recreational activities, but, for example, the application for licences or authorisations should only be necessary when it comes to an activity which is part of an important risk activity. But also — excuse for the coup — who will regulate the commission?, who will make it conform?, what representativeness will it have?, what will its functions be? Shouldn't there be a council that operated as a well-structured body for coordination and inter-administrative cooperation and public participation? How do you just outline it? This is indeterminate. This, like other elements of the law, leaves a wide margin of legal insecurity, very wide. To increase legal security, the law needs activities that are included in the law to be established, who is inside and who is outside, and this is not appreciated. His law is very abstract, very not concrete and very extensive, not by extensive exhaustive, not by extensive is realistic. And yes, it details a wide range of infringements and sanctions, very wide, with an incredible lack of proportionality, especially if we compare it with other autonomous communities. The sanctions system is huge. Of course, there are many agents not consulted by you who demand a real adequacy of the regulations to the basic criteria of the commercial standard, in this case the Code of Commerce, when defining what is a public establishment, because it is not defined; in definitions there is no discrimination between that it is a show of great format and small size. It doesn't make much sense for the Council to take such scrutiny of everything that will be done. It's good to check it out, but it would be more logical, in this state of affairs, to take care of mass events, not small ones. For that, the municipalities exist, and there must be a homogeneous regulation. We must save ourselves from the danger of recording with administrative procedures and economic sanctions activities that have nothing to do with offer and shows and recreational activities, but instead with aesthetic creation, with education, with the free exercise of intellectual criticism, production and collective knowledge. Nothing is said of this social fringe, for us of our first need. To finish, we say we need other ways, a need for different communication prior to the competent administrative authority by the administrations, much more agile, much simpler, and of course safeguarding the rights of access and above all the right of access to culture, which you in recent years have severely punished with brute cuts and with obvious consequences. Nothing else.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u7	Good morning. [[Applause]] It's an example for the working class, the way we have to go to conquer, to regain lost rights and freedoms, taken. Let's go to the preliminary draft debate. In fact, the BNG did not present a whole amendment, a refund amendment; if we present many amendments, because it seems to us to be a very better text, a text that has numerous deficiencies, weaknesses and contradictions, because it is also true that the reality that addresses this law is very complex, very different, and there is a pretense of solving issues that are certainly very different. So the first criticism we would have to make to you, Mr Rueda, is something that is very common in all the legal texts that bring to Parliament, which is the lack of transparency and information. You assume that there was a lot of transparency and a lot of consensus, but we certainly didn't see it in all the documentation, and because, besides, the more legislation there is about transparency and good government in reality less information and less participation we see. And, in addition, a participation that is restricted to certain professional groups that were actually given audiences — business associations in the hospitality sector, party rooms, verbenas and so on — in an apparent, formal participation, which then does not fit into the acceptance of their proposals and suggestions, to which they literally apply the roller. If this is called participation and consensus, of course we don't see it anywhere. And, above all, there was no information or participation directed to non-profit entities and groups, which will be very affected by the law in a negative sense if it is approved as it is. And it also seems to us, really, very controversial, and even contradictory to the words that you have expressed here, the justification for the need not to dictate to the Economic and Social Council, with the argument — you see — that regulated matters are not directly linked to Galicia's economic and social development. You have just said here that these are activities that have a great economic and social impact — their words in this audience — and it turns out your journalists say they are subjects that are not directly linked to Galicia's economic and social development and that public shows and related activities do not have a very important impact on the Greek economy. A second question that we want to point out and to what you have referred to, implicitly recognizing that the truth is that they haven't worked very much in these years—they have continued nine years in the Government—is the normative void. Of course, there is a normative vacuum, because, as you said, Galicia has exclusive competences in this matter, which are from 1996 and have passed more than twenty years and have not been able to make a specific standard, and so far there was regulation and there is still a very different and very disparate regulation that regulates all these issues. And other communities have applied and made their own legislation for years. It is also true that many times in response to tragedies, because, in the end, this issue is very directly related to tragic events that somehow force administrations to take action. So, you assume that you're often pioneers and the most modern and the most advanced, and we assume that it's not by accident that you haven't made your own rule, or we also assume that you don't expect others to approve them later copy them, don't you? Because that's also taking a lot here: to make a rule in Madrid and after you copy it here at the foot of the letter, as they did with animal welfare and with many others, and so they go out as they go out, that they have to be subjected to multiple amendments. We believe that the most important problem that the text of this law has — and that is why we think it is very better to expect that in the debate process it can be done — is the pretension — a really difficult intention to fit and resolve — of putting into this law very diverse realities, because the bill refers to a wide variety of activities, setting common obligations and requirements for very diverse and very disparate realities, and we want to draw attention to the need to clearly distinguish activities and shows that are clearly of a profit of other activities that organize over all associations that do not have this nature. And in this sense, it seems to us that it is a very better text, that we have to adapt it to the reality of Greece, that it is different from other realities and other territories, because we are a different country and we have a multiplicity, multitude, thousands of cultural and recreational activities and small shows every day -- we are at this time, for example, in the Magisteriums -- that they have a difficult fit into a law like this, although it is true that you made a last disposition that is a sort of chest of drawers, where they try to put in there almonds and partial solutions to fit these very diverse realities. But we think that this paragraph too is much better when it comes to verbenas or little shows, for example fewer than 100 people of assistance, who don't solve this issue. Another aspect in which we understand that the text of the law needs to be improved is what it has to do with the powers of the municipalities. This project, as has also happened in animal welfare, what it does is to transfer it and assign it to the municipalities that previously corresponded to the Galician Union, to the autonomous administration, for example with regard to the expansion and regulation of schedules, inspection or sanctions, and we really believe that this burden of competence can create many difficulties of application. You know perfectly well what the municipal reality of Galicia is, the diversity of municipalities in the country, and, if it's difficult to apply a rule like this to the cities of the country, imagine the small municipalities of less than 1,000 inhabitants like they're going to have to look at, for example, have to have a plan of inspection for this type of activity, unless, of course, the goal — that at best that is — is to outsource this type of control and inspection functions with companies that do this type of work. We think this kind of work has to be done by the Public Service. The report of the Local Administration Directorate-General itself refers precisely to this issue. "Whenever new powers are given, you have to provide for the necessary resources to ensure the financial adequacy of local authorities," but you do not resolve it in the text of the law. For example, when it comes to extending and reducing schedules on general time — Article 5(f) — which is currently autonomous competence and which will be passed to the municipalities; or authorizations for public shows and recreational activities of an extraordinary nature, which also becomes municipal competence. You introduce an article in which it is said that the Board and the deputies "can provide" support; to us that solution doesn't do us any good, and in the amendments that we present to you what we propose is that "they will have to lend", the Board and the Members "should lend", they can't "can lend", because, Counsellor, there are lots of councils that will not be able to do this inspection work anymore, because they don't even do it now, almost with difficulties, the larger councils. Another question — and I am not going to go into any other minor issues now — that seems to us important in this general debate is what has to do with the legal regime of administrative intervention, of what you have also talked about in your intervention, where we see a lot of contradictions and a lot of confusion, which, in addition, with this text, if it remains, of course it will be enlarged. This Parliament has approved -- I don't know if they have something that -- because they have this -- it's already customary -- Law 9/2013, of Galicia's economic entrepreneurship and competitiveness, to establish a legal regime for opening up economic establishments and activities, which has also been presented as the great panacea. [[Murmurs.]] Well, this law, which was going to solve all the problems, established a general regime of prior communication. In 2016 the Land Law and the Decree approving the Land Law Regulation, are again betting on the prior communication regime, and also in 2016 the regulation of that law of 2013, which again establishes the legal regime of prior communication as the general legal regime for economic activities. And, curiously, you now, in these bills, modify that legal regime and establish a new one; that is, this legal regime that was established in 2013, which was also established in the Land Law, in the Land Law Regulation, in the Rules of the Entrepreneurship Law, and that the councils have also begun to apply it from 2013, it turns out that they now change it in this law, establishing as a legal procedure the responsible declaration regime, with which we will have, Mr. Rueda, a genuine galimacy, a genuine guirigai of activities that will be subject to the responsible declaration regime and activities that will be subject to the prior communication regime. And this really creates a lot of insecurity, a lot of confusion, starting with the professionals working in the municipalities, for the private sector technicians and for citizenship in general, who won't know, for example, in the case of a coffee shop that has a bread order, that regime is going to apply, because here we have an economic activity on the one hand and a recreational activity on the other. What regime will apply in this case? It gives the impression that you in a legislative board in one way, in another, in another, in a contradictory way, you don't talk to each other, you don't coordinate with each other. So this question we think is one that needs to be reviewed, that needs to be corrected, by setting up a single regime, that we understand that, by clarity and by clarifying things for the people who work in this field, it would have to be what has been applied since 2013, which is that of previous communication, which is, in addition, what is also asked by Fegamp in his allegations. In addition, there is the galimatias and guirigai that you don't even clarify, because the General Emergency Directorate's report on the opportunity of the regulatory proposal says: "The preliminary draft refers in terms of the administrative intervention regime to the provisions of Galicia's Entrepreneurship and Competitiveness Act." Good. Then they make a unique last disposition that almost brings down half or more of that law. They state that they refer to the time it incorporates into the last provision a number of amendments; in particular, it establishes the declaration responsible as a general system of administrative intervention. This is a report from the Directorate-General for Emergency Affairs. Then the Economic and Financial Memory of the Directorate-General for Emergency Affairs says: "Consume the single legal regime for the exercise of activities in Galicia, this law collects prior communication as a general regime for administrative intervention." I mean, what general address do you have, that in a report says one thing and in the next it says the opposite? I think I should look at this, eh! Ladies and gentlemen, I come to this Parliament because I am aware that, after those days, questions arise, and it is appropriate to make joint reflections. It is true that we try to account for the state of the situation at all times: the set of the Greeks, through the media daily; the mayors of the municipalities concerned, with whom we continue in contact to assess the damage; the central government, which was also pending and present through the president of the Government and the Minister of the Environment; and also the set of political groups. But if I'm here, my own request and all of you, it's because I think that, after the emergencies, with a more precise balance of damage, with the necessary help for those already published and processed — which we will agree that it was the primary — is now when we have greater capacity to hold a debate with more knowledge and, therefore, a broader debate and a deeper debate. Ladies and gentlemen, the hundreds, thousands of Greeks, who lived in the first person the anguish of the fires, and above all the four families who mourn the loss of their loved one, deserve to be honored in this House with a debate going beyond the simple government-opposition. For my part, I come here today with the will that this Parliament can offer to them and to the whole of Galicia something more than just a session. We must offer answers to the success, but also answers to the future. The first question I think the Greeks are asking is, was it normal what happened? And, objectively, in our opinion, no, gentlemen. What we've been through was a completely exceptional situation. In an autonomous community like ours, unfortunately used to fight the fires, but also for that prepared and with the greatest experience of the entire Peninsula, many citizens will wonder how it is possible to state categorically that the Greeks will not live a wave of fires like that of 48 hours. Well, ladies and gentlemen, fire activity was neither normal nor the weather situation was normal. We certainly record extreme climatic conditions, and even some unpublished ones. It was extraordinary, and it continues to be extraordinary, the hydrological year in which we are living, with 35 percent less rain than normal, and, according to experts in this area, with the driest floor of the last sixty years. It was extraordinary and exceptional the relative humidity level, below 30% in many municipalities, where these values are only usual in summer — an example, Cervantes — and even reaching historical minimums in others like Kings Horses. The values of the temperatures were exceptional for a month of October, and very especially for that weekend, for example, more than 34 degrees in Ponteareas Town Hall. And to the extraordinary and exceptional, as I told you, something unusual began: the effects of hurricane Ophelia, the first category 3 that arrives in Galicia since there are records, which entered about 20 hours on Sunday and began leaving at 3-3.30 hours in the morning of Monday, and which was translated into extreme night temperatures — for example, more than 24 degrees in the municipality of Laza — and strong south winds, which especially in the night of 15 were over 100 kilometers/hour. This caused added difficulties relevant to the device, such as, for example, that helicopters could not fly, in addition to being late at night, they only did it in a short and very timely manner during the day, due to the low visibility generated by the fog. The flares complicated the control of the fires, also from the ground, or the fires from Portugal came to cross the natural border of the Mine River repeatedly, and with kilometric fronts, as happened in Lobies, in the Snows, in the Salvation of Mine or in the Mountains. Only in these three fires were more than 7,000 hectares burned. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, in order to evaluate correctly what happened, we must start from the basis that there are no comparable precedents; nor can it be equated to what was the worst year of this year in the period of the century or in 2006, where the wave of fires occurred at the time of the greatest risk and has been extended for many more days, nor is it homologable, for example, in October 2011, which also recorded a high incendiary activity. Unfortunately, this time, the weather conditions became the main ally of the incendiaries and fire in Galicia, as well as to a different extent in the Asturias, Castile and León and Portugal. At this point, I would like to reiterate Galicia's solidarity with the tragedy experienced in the country this year, with over 100 dead by forest fires. They know that as far as our possibilities are concerned, as we have already been in Portugal in the past, we will remain at their disposal. [[Applause]] The result in Galicia, according to the definitive assessment that the technicians transferred to us last week, was more than 49,000 hectares burned, 53% of the wooded mountain and 47% of the flat hill. This means it burned on a weekend more than in the previous three years, and that indicates the severity of the situation they had to deal with. So, for that reason, to underline, that with the conditions that have been given, although at last this 2017 is not the year with the most hectic acres of our history, it could perfectly be, and that is why I also want to value the work of the teams, who, with the wind, with the temperature, with the moisture and hurricane Ophelia, have achieved that around 2 out of 3 fires, of 264 those who have faced the two days, have been in contos, but above all have saved many lives that have been put at risk. In any case, the data on acres that point to the enormous damage that arsonists do are secondary, when we're talking about four people losing their lives. The burned hectares will be able to repair them in time, but unfortunately, what is unreparable is the damage of four families victims of incendiary terrorism. Here is the motivation of the second question, of the second question that I think it is for me to answer to the Greeks; a question that is repeated every summer, no doubt, but especially in the last few weeks, to the thread of the event: why does the mountain burn? Well, when it is repeatedly claimed that Galicia burns, it is already missing the truth. Galicia burns; mostly burns. It's not a personal opinion, which I share with so many Greeks, but the conclusion drawn by the people who investigate the fires and point out that on average 3 out of every 4 fires are not for natural causes and that most of them are intended. So they come to us historically communicating to successive governments. The first analyses point to this same trend as regards the 264 fires that arose in Galicia in 48 hours. Between Saturday and Sunday, 65% of the fires started at 7:30 p.m., the moment of greater risk for the impossibility of cutting them by air, since they cannot work from that moment on. And besides, in these days, a total of 19 fires required the activation of situation 2, which, as you know, is decreed when there is a risk of acceptance of population nuclei. To assess the exceptionalness of the nineteen situations 2, it should be borne in mind that until then throughout 2017, you will be included, if you had resorted to this situation 2 on three occasions, and in two days there were more level 2 fires than usually there are averages throughout the year. In the last five exercises, the average was 13. Ladies and gentlemen, those who set fire at night in various outbreaks, near the houses and in the weather conditions that there was, know that action can generate terror. If there was a plot, whether the incendiaries were organized or not, it is up to them to determine it to the police and the judicial authorities, who have previously ruled it out. But what I have said, what I say and what I repeat, I maintain and affirm with all the letters, without minimizing what they do or relativizing it, is that those who care indiscriminately against all and against all, who indiscriminately attack the natural and material heritage of people, who put human lives at risk and manage to end them, are not just individuals who by different circumstances have set fire to trees, but their acts, without measuring the consequences, are inherent to terrorists, and what they do has no other name than incendiary terrorism. We have to put an end to the incendiaries, and we have to end the incendiary terrorism, and we do little favor as a country if we start to lose importance or even frizzy with who I am or what they do. [[Applause]] When it comes to the safety and well-being of citizens, there is a problem of public order which, first, we must condemn with all constancy and which, second, we must try to punish accordingly. Another thing that's different, but that's why it doesn't stop being serious, is that there are also fires caused by neglect. Logically, throwing a hood or making a burn in days in which they are forbidden is not the same as intentionally setting fire in the mountain, but the consequences can become the same. So it doesn't mean that we should be condescending with practices that equally threaten the safety and well-being of citizens and our natural heritage, and that's why we must continue to insist on utmost caution. A fire that goes out of his hands can have mitigating, no doubt, but it also has the responsibility to put us all in enormous danger. Ladies and gentlemen, the third question I think the Greeks are asking is, how did you respond to the incendiary activity during these days, which actually placed it in the face of an indiscriminate and difficult attack? Despite the quantitative means that were used in the work of extinction, with a device that was the most numerous of any month of October of our history, the determination of the different administrations involved and despite the heroism of all who fought against the fire, the main difficulty resulted from not only paying attention to the natural environment, but also burning very intensely in the inhabited nuclei; that is, subjecting to the great risk of many human lives. The best illustration of this gives us the data from the 112 emergency center, which has been strengthening its staff to more than 150% in times of greater crisis intensity, as it has outpaced the maximum activity logs of the entire decade. Over the next two days more than 9,000 calls were held, almost twice as many as an ordinary day. Ladies and gentlemen, in the face of the proliferation of fires near the houses, we saw, especially for 12 hours on Sunday night, the obligation to put people's protection before the protection of the forest land. I myself was present at the focal point when the technicians decided to bring down most of the agents of the mountains, even to completely urban places where the Union has no direct competition, but we, of course, decided to act. I have no doubt that any of you would make exactly the same decision in a similar situation, because the priority was, they had to be, the Greeks and the Greeks; it was to prevent you from re-editing here a misfortune like the one we would live just a few months before in Pedrógrão Grande, in Portugal, and how sadly they once again lived those same days in the neighboring country. That weekend there were up to 19 simultaneous fires in urban areas and urbanized with houses, up to Vigo's own urban centre. Of them, five have affected natural spaces. Neither fires nor incendiaries distinguished rural, urban, species, natural park, ordered mountain or not. Ladies and gentlemen, we prioritized people on the device and also prioritized them in the responses, which we understand should be immediate, especially for the four families who lost a loved one, for the people who were injured and for those who lost homes and businesses. And the fourth question lies: do we act with utmost diligence with the help? In the first 24 hours, we adopted the Framework Decree, which provides legal support for aid; in the following seven days, we authorized the order which is specific and enables them, with a share of more than 20 million euros — which can be extended, logically, if necessary — and opened a free telephone service at the disposal of those affected to the east throughout the process. Techniques responsible for processing applications are instructed to give special preference to aid intended to alleviate individual damage; that is, mainly to compensation to which the families of the victims of death — who, in addition, know that they have been strengthened — or to the expenses to which the injured may have incurred, with the daily limit set in order. Similarly, we have already activated aid for people who have been affected by their housing, as well as the household allowance, up to 100% of the value of repair in case of habitual housing and 40% in occasional residences, and by completing, in addition, temporary housing costs in cases where this was necessary. I announce to you that we will include an amendment to the Law on Administrative and Tax Measures so that the aid included in this decree is exempt from taxation on income taxes to physical persons in which the autonomic process refers. Over the past few days, there has also been considerable progress in the records of various commercial, commercial and industrial establishments that have been affected by fires, and for them we have enabled two measures: first, advice to help those affected to recover their businesses; and second, and most importantly, aid for recovery or reconstruction works of up to 600,000 euros per establishment, in addition to loans with interest subsidies to finance these actions, compatible with future State aid, as well as insurance that they have contracted. Within the productive sectors, too, logically, special attention must be given to rural areas and the sea. In the first area — in rural areas — it is necessary to repair damage such as private ownership infrastructures, forest clues, water captures, closures, beers and orchards; burnt wood costs, prioritising collection at the light border, in order to prevent the spread of the pine rain; repair of goods, machinery or equipment; total or partial loss of agricultural production of the farms; loss of livestock or loss of herds of livestock and apicolas; or for the acquisition of supplementary food for the extended livestock or the herds in the affected areas. For its part, the Council of the Sea also keeps an eye on the sea bankers until the continence that serious damage could occur. In addition — and as we have already discussed at the moment — we have approved aid for other needs: re-approaching hot tourist signals; recovering sports infrastructure; repairing damaged roads, especially in the province of Pontevedra, whose works are already being developed; or supporting the owners of the ordered cinematic lands that have been affected. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to stop at one of the most important games we have set aside in order, which are the city councils' aids; you help both to compensate for the expenses arising from the actions that were necessary to deal with the fires, to repair damage caused by municipal property and rights, and that we have at our disposal to give them the necessary support, along with the deputies, as we agreed at the meeting held together. We have already informed the Galaga Federation of Municipalities and Provinces directly, and we are awaiting the extent of the damage to the municipalities concerned. Let me take this opportunity to make a call for this response to be made with as much speed as possible, because it depends on the fact that the aid enabled in Galicia can be complementary at national level and, in its case, at European level; that is, with the support of the Central Government — which has already confirmed that this month will be approved — and also with the European Union Solidarity Fund, which we want to explore with the rest of the communities, but for what we need to have a comprehensive and complete evaluation of the costs as soon as possible. So, the same commitment of the Union, with resources and with speed, I ask all the administrations involved so that the damage caused by the fires can be compensated as soon as possible. On this objective, too, there is a great relevance to the third pillar of the performances. I'm referring to the tasks of regeneration and consolidation of the burned soil. As a matter of urgency, we started fixing the affected floors, in order to prevent them from drifting and the arrival of sediments at rivercourses and streams; a goal that we do not close and in which we will continue to work. We also prioritized the response in protected natural spaces, especially the restoration of habitats in Lower Limita-Xurés and Anchores, due to the presence of species catalogued in some cases in danger of extinction. And besides, we've begun to develop the reforestation roadmap as well, with the aim of acting in a first phase in the 32 fires of more than 300 hectares that took place throughout 2017 and in a second phase in the rest of the places where technicians see need and more urgent action. Ladies and gentlemen, to recognize the exceptional nature of the fire wave that justifies this appearance, to have the aid guaranteed and underway for those affected and to start the recovery work on damaged land should not make it obvious to us the need to review the more structural areas linked to fires, whether in the forest and environmental environment or in the discharge of criminal action. To the reflections in this two-fold area we refer to the final part of this appearance. If Galicia has never been satisfied with this, neither after this wave of fires can we go out thinking it's all right. Everything can be improved and, of course, also forest planning — they must be perfected — to reduce our exposure to fires as much as possible, despite the fact that zero risk does not exist as long as there are fires or it is not possible in a community like ours, with more than 2 million acres of mountains. Ladies and gentlemen, counting a forest policy that makes the mountains more profitable and lessens the risk of fires was a commitment by our Government before October and will continue to be after and throughout the legislation. I believe that in recent years decisions have been made of great importance in this regard. Let me make a brief reference to ten of those decisions already taken. Since 2012, Galicia has brought into force the first Mountain Law in our history adopted in this Parliament. In 2011, we launched the Decree to encourage forest owners' groups. We require all mountain owners to have a management plan from 2020 onwards. We regulate the obligation to reinvest the revenue obtained by the joint neighbouring mountains in actions of improvement and forest protection. Also, there are clear restrictions on land reclassification, which, on the basis of the existing national legislation, has been banned since 2015 for thirty years. We create help for native species plantations, like chestnut trees. We put the Galaga Forest Industry Agency into operation for the first time. And above all -- like the eighth measure -- we approved the most ambitious tax policy in Galicia's rural history, first, because in 2016 we approved zero taxes for any purchase, sale or exchange of a farm in rural; a measure that has already benefited 20,600 Greeks, and also because since 2017 the same zero tax policy applies to the purchase of housing in rural areas for certain collectives and income levels. And finally, because, as it appears in the budget project that the House has, for the next year, we are introducing tax incentives for those people who are engaged in economic activity in rural areas, in this case with deductions both in the income tax of physical persons and in the property tax. Ladies and gentlemen, all these measures — these ten measures — make it clear that in recent years we have taken the witness of previous governments, who have also been aware since the beginning of democracy, that ordering the mountains, putting them in value, is one of the most important challenges we have and that we continue to have as a country. Neither before nor now do we give that task to the end; they summon us today but also summon us in the future. And neither before nor now do we consider that the responsibility of a government is exclusive; it calls on this Parliament and also calls on all citizens of Galicia. So, ladies and gentlemen, in the same way that we have been able to reach important agreements over the last few years, I suggest that the shared conviction that in the forest sector we must continue to work on focusing also on a single offensive. For months now, with the start of the new legislation, the Union has resumed its design of the new forest planning and impulse measures that we consider necessary. Most of them are already in progress and processing; some of them are included in the Law on Administrative and Tax Measures which is currently being dealt with by this Parliament and which is therefore open to their arrivals, and others must be approached together with the Galaga Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, as they directly influence on the day-to-day local administration. And there's a last package of measures, such as those that make up the new Forest Plan, which we're assessing with all the agents involved and we also want to close down with the parliamentary groups. The future forest, ladies and gentlemen, depends not only on the government of every moment, but on the whole country, and for decades. To hit or not is not only in the hands of the Executive, because getting us all involved — you too as a whole of citizens — is everyone's responsibility. I believe, therefore, that the best way to create a collective consciousness that protects, orders and values the mountain is to start from here in this Parliament. And that's why I propose, in line with what the different groups of the House have expressed and broadening their objectives, the creation of a specific commission to seek a country agreement on forest policy that protects the unit of criterion and the unit of action. [[Applause]] In addition to this specific commission in the Chamber, I have a package of 30 measures — half of them already being processed — in which I propose them to work together on three distinct but convergent fronts: firstly, on the issue of prevention; secondly, on the issue of management; and thirdly, on the issue of socio-economic dynamization. Ladies and gentlemen, as I have already pointed out, incendiary activity is directly related to a problem of public order which is proving that it endangers the safety of citizens, and therefore preventing it is essential. To this day, 3 out of every 4 euros in the fight against fire are already going for that purpose, and our vocation is to continue to act in this field even more intensely. In fact, last September we approved the help to have the mountains in the best possible conditions towards the next fire campaign in 2018. In total, in this first delivery, we approved at the September Meeting of the Board of Boards 11 million euros for individual or collective owners of the mountains to do forestry work or other preventive treatments. I believe that all the Greeks must recognize that the instructions for the prevention and cleaning of mountains are already quite definite, regardless of whether it can be fine-tuned at any particular point. The main problem is that on many occasions there is a remarkable resistance to compliance. When the mountain owners -- and we don't forget that 98% of the mountains in Galicia are private -- we don't assume that we have to manage them periodically, there's a problem. When the municipalities do not comply with their competence to inspect the state of the farms near the inhabited nuclei, whether it is inappropriate or not to sanction or to do those tasks and subsequently repertoire the cost, there is also a problem. And when administrations don't address the problem of abandoned plots or without known owner, and even more if they're in the perimeters of the urban nucleus, there's also a problem. Well, ladies and gentlemen, in the face of this triple problem that concerns us all, I present to you the following proposals that, in my opinion, we could start implementing next 2018. First. To advance in an agreement with the local administration to economically encourage the cleaning of the mountains, especially in the secondary lanes, that is, in the surrounding tracks of the inhabited nuclei, so that the owners who delegate these jobs to the Town Hall pay a fixed price, equal to a basic dew, and the administrations cover the remaining part where necessary. That meant a very high public disbursement in the early years for cleaning the farms that take longer to the ground and in which, logically, it is more expensive that cleaning, but in which virtually zero cost would be reached for administrations in a few exercises, if we act systematically. Second. To guarantee the total involvement of the municipalities, which have direct competence; and therefore to exercise them cannot depend on the will of each and every concrete exercise. In this sense, we will propose to the Fegamp a formula so that 100% of the rural municipalities that do not have their own fire department contains forest brigades, that we co-finance them since June, in front of the 195 municipalities that in this season have forest brigades and that have signed the corresponding agreement in 2017; but they must not be 195, they must be close to 300. It is important, not only because it enables the extinguishing device to be reinforced when necessary, but because — we do not forget — the main work of these brigades before the most risky campaign is precisely prevention. In the same line, we assure you that the Rural Plan 2018 will be directed exclusively and for the first time to rural municipalities to hire personnel dedicated to forestry, mountain cleaning and other forest valuation tasks; and we make available to them 7 million euros. With the same objective of strengthening collaboration with municipalities and Members, they also know that we have been working on a new emergency map that incorporates and coordinates into the single command several progress made in recent years, such as the comarcallization of fire stations — which is already defined as a group set up specifically for this — the Supreme Emergency Groups — the GHGs — which we defend to consolidate as a body focused solely on security tasks; the new technology tools of the digital emergency network; or the operation of a telephone for mayors, which has been requested these days since the Fegamp for emergency cases, and that we already confirm that we will enable them. Of all this, by the way, they will receive information from the Vice-President of the Union with an appearance, at his own request, which will be requested for the next plenary session. Similarly, as already mentioned in the Law on Administrative and Tax Measures, we intend to increase the legal certainty of the municipalities in order to facilitate the subsidiary execution of preventive measures in the case of unknown ownership parcels. We clarify that in these cases there is no need for judicial authorization to access the management of these, and we allow the Administration to sell the resulting wood to compensate for the damage. And to address the third problem, that of the abandoned lands, we are also working on several measures, considering that, according to the data in the catastrophe, it is estimated that in Galicia there are more than 300,000 shares of unknown holder and an even greater number of people who have already died or cannot be located. Therefore, to act on them is to act in a very important part of the rural environment, which at this moment is without anyone looking at it. As a general rule, in the wake of the agreement we signed with the Ministry of Business last 2013, we propose to incorporate in a cautious and immediate manner those farms on which we are investigating whether they have an unknown owner, deceased or imlocalizable; that is, we are going to put them, as long as the research is open, at the disposal of those who want to work. The same purpose, which is incorporated into the Land Bank, is to give the estates abandoned on the neighboring mountains in common, in case the corresponding community does not want to take care of them. And the same process of mobilising abandoned land could be extended to other purposes, such as the impulse of agricultural polygons; that is, we can mobilize farms incorporated into the Land Bank when there is a business demand that undertakes to give them the right profitability. I would ask you to support these eight measures of a more general nature, which, in any case, we must supplement with more specific ones, which are already included in the Law of Measures to be adopted in December, and I refer to some of these cases. It says the law that we establish protection tracks around electrical substations and telecommunications stations. So far there has been only this obligation on the lines of transport and distribution of electricity and gas. Also, it must be forced to manage biomass in isolated rural dwellings, even if they are more than 400 metres from the mountain; to extend the limitations in secondary ranges, that is, within 50 metres around the buildings, where there can be no acacias or pyrophytic species, basically pines and eucalyptus, in certain areas that are at greater risk of fires by altitude or wind or in campings, petrol stations and installations with flammable material; to tighten the requirements for planting pine trees and eucalyptus between 30 and 50 metres around the nuclei, where we intend to extend the planting distance to 10 meters between specimens. In addition, I think it is necessary to increase the citizen's knowledge of all these protection measures, and with this aim we are going to carry out a dissemination campaign that will give all the Greek people the obligations and responsibilities we have as owners. I do not want to finish this paragraph without mentioning one of the most necessary measures for prevention, especially in the medium and long term; I refer to education for younger generations. To raise awareness about the mountains, we intend to launch campaigns that allow 100% of the boys to reach before they finish compulsory teaching. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, 14 measures aimed at more prevention and therefore less risk; some of them would already come into force on January 1st. That's the way we have to endure in the first place. The second field of action, as I told you, is linked to the sorting of our mountains. We're also working on it, and we've got the hand to do it together. The main work we have ahead of us is to continue to complete the review of the Galician Forest Plan; an obvious necessity given that the current was designed more than two decades ago and therefore needs to be updated. They know that we have already moved forward in this work by the Galician Forest Council with several relevant objectives, such as to increase decisively the area occupied by native leafy trees — Castilian, oak and deer — to promote groups of forest owners with tax advantages such as those already included in the next year's budget, or also to approve tax incentives for forest entrepreneurs. However, with this first base and after it is evaluated at the Forest Council as appropriate, I inform you that we will send the Forest Plan to Parliament so that it can also be enriched by the arrivals of parliamentary groups. Galicia will be grateful if we agree on a forest consensus plan that is most useful for the next four decades. In this paragraph, ladies and gentlemen, there is a second level of action in the mountains: district level management plans, which we are also pushing. The goal is to have for the mountains instruments similar to the urban plans of the municipalities; that is, as a general urban management plan guides urban growth in municipalities, these plans will guide the forest growth of the mountains of each district. Out of the 19 districts in Galicia, the proposal will begin in January by the province of Ourense, which has 5, and by the province of Pontevedra, which has 4, because it is those that have the greatest affect and incendiary activity. Another measure is the general management of the Forest Plan; district management. But there must also be a third level of management in each individual owner or association. It is provided for in the current legislation, which stipulates that all mountains or forest lands must have a forest management or management tool available in 2020 so that they can be authorised for use. Our commitment is to accompany the Greeks in this process and, in fact, we have already made available to the owners for months, the first 4 million euros so they can meet their obligations by 2020. This process has a great deal to do with the plan for improving areas of extensive pastoral care in the provinces of Ourense and Pontevedra, which already, as we move forward in the debate on the state of Autonomy, has a device of 1.3 million euros for this year. It's contrasting its impact, and that leads us to facilitate this activity. Ladies and gentlemen, these four proposals that I have just mentioned must be complemented by two other proposals which have also been advanced in recent years, but I think they are historical pending matters in Galicia and which therefore takes time to reverse. The most important thing is partial restructuring and, in this sense, I think we should encourage as much as possible certain procedures that must greatly speed up the process. Considering that there are no taxes on farms in rural areas, no agricultural or forestry, the concentrations made by individuals are much faster, less conflictive and with zero taxes and zero costs. Especially attractive are concentrations in small-surface areas, such as those that we are piloting in a parish in Sarria, 300 hectares, or in Oimbra, 1,000 hectares, based also on permutations among individuals. Similarly, it is also necessary to deepen the administrative simplification already initiated with the permission of cuts. There is no doubt that the previous process, with more than six phases of authorization, led to an important slowness, which will in good manner revert to the Act of Promoting the Implementation of Entrepreneurship Initiatives, establishing a single window and therefore a single application, and therefore a unique authorisation for species that need greater protection. I mean the native species. It's the only responsible statement for the pines and eucalyptus. But for this to be really operational, we're opening a technology platform at the Joint Portal with the geolocalized and properly updated information that lets you know when the mountains are part of spaces that are subject to some protection regime, so that operators can have more respectable forest management planning. Ladies and gentlemen, preventive measures, management measures and now dynamisation measures. Generating courage in the mountains and avoiding abandonment is our best defense. A productive, exploited mountain that the community perceives as useful is a less vulnerable mountain against the fire. That is why I think the third pillar of the roadmap that I present to you is precisely to support the socio-economic aspect of the forest. We are already talking about a sector of great economic and social importance: EUR 2 billion per year invoicing, 80,000 people employed directly or indirectly in it and some 3,000 forestry-related companies. For this challenge, the Galaga Forest Industry Agency, which has just become operational, will have a key role to play both to encourage industry pemmes more intensively and to promote the manufacture of consumer products, as to make the sector more competitive through technological renewal and the application of engineering, as well as to promote quality employment. In this latter aspect, in employment, we will announce that next year we will devote 5 million specific to forestry workshops exclusively for forestry treatments, biomass management and community-based neighbors and individuals and partners. With the launch of this programme, it would be possible for unemployed people to obtain a training contract that enables them to gain certifiable knowledge in the sector and, at the same time, help in cleaning up the mountains of our community. Similarly, the Rural Environment Council will also address the amendment of the decree on forestry development societies with the aim of simplifying current legislation and facilitating the entry of new investors. The same objective of attracting entrepreneurs to the forest sector is, as I said, in the tax cuts introduced into the Accompaniment Act, both in the income tax and in the equity tax, which we include, I insist, if the budgets go forward, from January 2018, so that anyone who wants to invest in the rural sector and, therefore, in the forest sector, knows that it will have greater tax facilities. Ladies and gentlemen, I have just told you a series of 25 measures; 25 measures to improve prevention, management and placement in the value of our mountains. Some in the short term, though also initiatives that will not yield immediate results, but I am convinced that the generations of Greeks that are yet to come will thank us in the future that we leave behind part of our differences to address this matter. What needs to continue to intensify prevention? Of course. What is needed in the area of management? Of course. What is needed to increase the value of our forest heritage? Of course. What is necessary to raise the demand and control for everyone, administrations and individuals to do their duty? Of course. What must we try to improve coordination between administrations and we all get the most involved in our possibilities? Of course. What should be sought for an even more effective response to one of Europe's best extinction services? Of course. That there are species that burn faster than others? Of course. But, ladies and gentlemen, the work that still has to be done, the improvements that still need to be achieved, and the opposition-government confrontation between parties confuse us. Galicia won't suffer the same without incendiaries. All the efforts made and what they're about to do will do nothing if we don't cut down the real and last responsible ones of this scourge: the incendiaries. We need to see how the fires don't inflict the biggest blow, which is to lead human lives ahead; they hurt those people who saw their place reduced to ashes; they hurt those families who saw how the work of decades could be lost in a matter of seconds; they indignate us that it seems that we have to assume as normal and routine the fact that, whenever it does not rain, Galicia has to burn, that there are people who are waiting for the weather conditions to be as good as possible for them to do as much harm as possible; and they force us to know that in the vast majority of times what puts the natural heritage at risk, the belongings of many people and even other people's life is neither chance nor bad fortune. We are angry at the fact that there are terrorist incendiaries, even terrorists, who use the fire to change public peace in Galicia and provoke episodes of terror such as those which, unfortunately, we all lived two weeks ago. Galicia doesn't deserve to cause us that pain.
