Galicia is full of fires, but more and more of the incendiaries. Let's go get them, and they'll pay for what they did. The incendiaries must answer to justice for their crimes, and for that it is evident that they must be brought before them. Many times for an incendiary to go to trial, it is necessary that virtually impossible conditions be given, unless they are caught infringingent. Today I'm going to present you with a series of measures to increase your chances of doing so, to increase your chances of taking them infringinging. First of all, we propose the creation of the forest research teams, which will expand and allow us to specialize in the work that has so far been carried out by the forest fire research brigades. This specialized body will be dedicated to research at the point of origin of the fires, analysis of the relationship between the fires and the activities that take place on the mountain and the follow-up of the burned areas. This work is essential to support the research work of state forces and security bodies, which is to which they belong. There will be a minimum of three officers from the forest research teams in each of the 19 forest districts; that is, Galicia will have an environment of 50-60 agents dedicated to this purpose. Secondly, the non-compliance monitoring teams will be set up to ensure that the measures included in the forest and fire defence regulations are complied with, both those already in force and those already included in the Budget Accompanying Act, and those we approve, in their case, in that agreement. These teams will be responsible for monitoring safety distances, in addition to supporting municipalities in including biomass management tracks to defend inhabited nuclei. In addition, they will also locate careless and abandoned plots or that do not comply with the regulations. And there will be a minimum of two surveillance troops for each forest district, around 40. The professionals of the forest research teams and the professionals of the non-compliance surveillance teams will share a unique command in the figure of the provincial coordinator. Thirdly, we are going to set up the Galician Forest Fire Investigations Administrative Register. In it we will systematically store all data and reports and assessments of administrative investigations into forest fires. This log will be accessible by all research teams and by state security forces and bodies, so that they have more information and better coordination. In addition, we propose, fourthly, in this area, the creation of a forest surveillance network, which, through video surveillance cameras and proportionally to the incendiary activity of each place, helps to identify the incendiary activity and detect targets as easily as possible. This, these facilities, will be done at the locations that the public operator Retegal has. For that, we will take advantage of the experience we have of some pilot evidence that has been carried out over the last few years. And finally, measure number five is strengthening a knowledge campaign on phone number 085, so that it's, in addition to the number to warn from the sighting of fires, also from the sighting of the incendiaries. These five initiatives will facilitate the task of identifying the incendiaries at the same time as they are committing the crime; therefore, they will allow it to be easier to take them to the judge. However, I also consider it essential to provide the justice of instruments to give the arsonists a response proportional to the enormous damage they do. I am aware that the latest reform of the Criminal Code has already intensified the response given to these criminals; by the way, a proposal made by the Government of the Galician Union and adopted at the Congress of Deputies. But I also hope that we agree that in the fire strike it was clear that all forest fires brought a potential risk to people's lives. Its perpetrators must be judged accordingly. This will be one of the reflections that I will bring to the top that we will celebrate with Asturias and Castile-Leon in the first days of December. It is necessary to establish the discontinuity of the forest masses so that the fires produced are not uncontrolled. Four. It is necessary to promote and recover the agricultural model that generates life and wealth in rural areas; to stop the forestry of agricultural land; to maintain, respect and increase the distances of security; to mobilize agricultural land; to place the PDR and the CAP firmly at the service of the productive economy; to reopen, maintain and revitalize agricultural extension offices; to count on mountain communities; to seek part management. Finally, it is necessary to fully manage the territory, the rural environment and the urban environment, putting an end to the speculative uses of the ground. Urbanism is not territorial planning. It's necessary to make a single impression...

It also points to the Galician Natural History Society that any project or use of renewable energy sources — even if it is financed directly or indirectly from public funds — must necessarily demonstrate neutral or positive effects on biodiversity, natural spaces and socio-economic aspects. G.P.'s non-legislative proposal. People of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr. Gonzalo Trenor López and seven other Members, on the development by the Government of Galicia, in 2017, of plans for the use and management of the six natural parks of Galicia and the National Park of the Atlantic Islands
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u170	This motion for a non-law has been tabled amendments by the Parliamentary Group of En Marea, Socialist and the Nationalist Block of Galicia.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u171	Mr Trenor has the floor to make a non-law proposal.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u172	Ladies and gentlemen, Galicia has an area of 29,574 square kilometers. In Greek territory we have a great diversity of natural means, thanks to our position, our geography, our orography, but also the territory reflects our own action, in some cases to improve, enrich what nature has given us, but in others, unfortunately not so much. We have more than 1,200 kilometers of back, the altitude varies between sea level and sea level to its maximum altitude of 2,124 meters in Pena Trevinca, not forgetting seabed. The variety of climates that provide ocean environments, mountain environments, even Mediterranean ones, and a multitude of microclimates that give rise to a multitude of ecosystems, all of them different and special, that are the result of natural evolution and how to take advantage of natural resources. The Protected Natural Space Galeg Network represents more than 12% of the total area of Galicia. It draws up the natural spaces of the autonomous community which have a special protection regime under the various autonomous, state or Community regulations, as well as international conventions. These natural spaces collect the most representative and significant ecosystems of the Greek territory: coasts and rivers, ponds, river ecosystems, greenhouses of the interior, forests and forests, etc., and the autonomic regulations governing their protection are collected in the Nature Conservation Act. Within these natural spaces stand out those who have unique elements of fauna, flora, biology and the representativeness of their ecosystems or their scientific and educational value and interest, also by the beauty of their landscapes or by their cultural or historical projection, which require special attention and make them worthy of a protection figure, which is that of natural parks. At this time in Galicia we have declared six natural parks: the Fragas do Eume, Corrubedo and Lagoas of Carregal and Vixán, in the province of Coruña; The Winterfell and the greenhouse of the Lastra and the Lower Limia and the Serra do Jurés, in the province of Ourense; and Mount Aloia, in Pontevedra. In order to facilitate a coordinated management of the spaces that make up it, as well as to contribute to its recognition and dissemination, it has been created as an integrated and unitary system of all natural parks to the Galician Natural Parks Network. It has as its main objectives to transform the natural parks of our community into a shared and coordinated management model, promote cooperation and promote common objectives, empower natural parks as a value of economic development and dynamize the local economies of its surroundings, also know the data and figures related to the management of the main areas of action on the Natural Parks Network, place the Natural Parks of Galicia brand as a reference in Spain under a global corporate identity, capitalize Galicia's wide and diverse natural heritage through the promotion of natural parks and enhance the uniqueities of each natural park to convert the Network into a green Spain icon and as natural spaces of interest in Europe. The Galician Natural Parks Network has fined coordination, among them, but also with other protected networks or spaces, especially with the National Park of the Atlantic Islands, but also with management and use and promotion and value. The Law of Natural Heritage and Biodiversity establishes the basic legal regime of conservation, sustainable use, improvement and restoration of natural heritage and its biodiversity. It provides for the development of plans for the use and management of natural parks, corresponding to their approval by the competent body of the Autonomous Community. Even so, it states that the governing plans will prevail over urban plans. The Galician Autonomy Statute takes exclusive competence to dictate additional rules for the protection of the environment and the landscape, in accordance with the Constitution. The Galician Nature Conservation Act sets out natural park planning instruments for natural resources management plans (PORNs) and management and management plans. The concept of repertoire plans provides for the development of the guidelines issued in the management plans in these documents, setting out the management plans for the administration's actions in the field of the application of natural parks. In accordance with this regulation, during this year 2017, the development of the Rector Plans has already been implemented, both of the natural parks and of the National Maritime-Terrestrial Park of the Atlantic Islands. With regard to the Master Plan for the use and management of the Natural Park of the Atlantic Islands of Galicia, it is necessary to stress that, after the start of the processing, in 2011, there were substantial changes that allowed for a thorough review of the preliminary document which was exposed to public information, and the same received during the previous process, in the process of public processing, a total of 25 rounds. In this process, at the same time, the National Parks Act is approved and, in subsequent adaptation of the National Parks Director Plan, it is indicated that the management and use master plans will be adjusted to the National Parks Network Director Plan. With these new regulations, the General Directorate of Natural Heritage has carried out the revision of the document in accordance with the new requirements. In this framework, work has been carried out to improve the zoning according to the new studies carried out in recent years, and at this time the opening document of the National Park's Master Plan for Use and Management will be submitted to public participation in general, in accordance with Law 27/2006, so that the rights of access to information, public participation and access to justice in environmental matters are regulated. The purpose of this participatory process is that the document can be enriched by suggestions and observations made. With regard to plans for the use and management of natural parks, the draft Eume Fragas Rector is currently being updated and revised. With regard to the documents of use and management of the rest of the five natural parks in Galicia, it should be noted that they are currently in processing, since the Board has contracted the contract for the technical advisory service in the preparation of the documentation of these plans, giving rise to previous tasks of measurement and land verification. It is expected that during the second half of 2018 the procedures for approving the decrees of the governing plans for the use and management of the six natural parks of Galicia will be completed. This situation shows that the Union is working on a document that guarantees the conservation of Galicia's natural heritage while taking advantage of it and the economic, social and cultural development of the municipalities in which they sit and their closest surroundings. These are projects where they confuse a lot of actors, so it's necessary to respond to all the sensitivity and needs. This will be done during the drafting process of the drafts, so at that time information rounds, talks, newspapers and meetings will be started to present the content and bring the proposals closer together, as well as to recompile the considerations presented by social agents and park owners. The arrivals will be part of the document that is passed as a decree and will regulate the uses and management of natural parks. The management and use plan of each park will be an instrument that, as far as possible, must be endorsed by the greatest consensus of all sectors involved, for what is concerned is that it is a plan respected by all those who participate in one way or another in the National Park and the natural parks. In his parliamentary appearance the request to explain the objectives of the Council for this legislation, the Environment Adviser announced the development and approval of the plans for the use and management of all parks, which he agrees with in the electoral program with which the People's Party of Galicia competed for the 2016 autonomous elections. But this group wants to extend this commitment to the Greek society to the rest of the political groups, reaching a broad political agreement to promote this immediate action. In relation, ladies and gentlemen, with your amendments, our offer to reach a broad political agreement and a firm offer, and we understand that some of the points of your amendments can be incorporated into your initiative, so at the end of this exhibition we will try to approach positions and advance towards the transaction of a joint initiative among all groups. Nothing else and thank you very much.

People of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr Jaime Castineira Broz and six other Members, on the momentum of the Galician Union in the development of a Galician rehabilitation law

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u189	To formulate the non-law proposition has the word Mr. Castineira.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u190	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen. Galicia has numerous rural nuclei and urban areas with great historical or heritage value that are uninhabited or damaged. In order to dignify the landscape built and enhance traditional architecture and also in order to generate economic activity and development, the Greek Government wants to promote the recovery of these damaged areas so that they function as centres of economic, social or cultural activity. Last May, the President of the Board and on the second day of this month, the Infrastructure and Living Adviser in Commission 3rd, during the presentation of the Council's budgets, announced that they were working on Galicia's First Rehabilitation, Regeneration and Urban Renewal Act, a legal tool that will facilitate and enhance the recovery of the building and make it possible for Galicia to continue to maintain its own identity. They indicated as a primary goal to improve the living conditions of residents, to recover urban and rural areas from our community that are in a state of deterioration and to encourage the culture of rehabilitation. For from the People's Parliamentary Group we believe that this law must include, among other things, the promotion of the culture of rehabilitation; that is, a owner or owner of a building — and the rest of citizenship — feel the need and value the profitability of recovering the already built. For this, it is essential to reduce barriers that currently hinder rehabilitation and urban regeneration: legislative, administrative and management barriers, property acquisition, housing, social, demographic and, of course, financial. Legislative barriers. Home, there is currently no specific legal framework for rehabilitation in the Autonomous Community. Regular legislation is dispersed and not encouraging rehabilitation; it requires a clear legal framework that serves as a guide and reference. Administrative and management standards. Getting licenses right now — licenses, permits or authorizations — to rehabilitate an building, especially if it's in a core or in a historical shell, is a real hate. Honestly, they discourage individuals and companies from embarking on rehabilitation processes. That's the reality. Maybe I'll talk to them because I'm from Lugo and a license there, the normal thing is that it takes at least a year. But it is a case that is unfortunately being given in other cities and places. So we have to try to get a law that turned this situation around. More often, it's very common to find people -- I tell them from my experience how they look -- or people who have just rehabilitated a building in the historical building, and when they're finished, they see a building that's pretty close, they congratulate them and they say, "Yes, but if it's today, I don't start." That is a reality, and that is an obligation on us as Members when we present here the new Rehabilitation Act, that Act of rehabilitation and urban regeneration, trying among all of us to make the best law possible so that this will never happen again. People say, "Yes, it's a pleasure, and if I came back, I'd start again." We have to change and turn that trend around, and that's a reality we need, because at this moment it often takes longer to get the necessary permissions and licenses than to do the works, and that can't be. And I'm not blaming anyone, it's the system we've got right now and that's been coming for years. You need a law that regulates this situation and clears the steps and marks the right guidelines. Themes of habitability, barriers of habitability. It is common in historical helmets that there are low-sized buildings or with different degrees of level of protection that do not meet the standards of living. So it is necessary to seek — even though there are flexible living standards and if we are trying to find meeting points — a balance between the level of protection and the level of housing in order to carry out these rehabilitations. Social. It's a reality that there are many historical helmets or nuclei that are at the moment, apart from architecturally deteriorated, also from the social point of view; that is, they are marginal neighborhoods where there are practically no inhabitants or are semi-inhabited, and therefore they need an intervention I would say that from the social, economic and urban point of view and with appropriate management models to recover not only the buildings but also the neighborhood and the life of that neighborhood or that area. Financial measures? Of course, because all of this requires important financial measures. And rehabilitation is nothing new and I'm not going to say anything new, which is a situation in which to rehabilitate a building comes out in many cases twice as expensive as making a new house, not the same, but twice or even more. And if we're talking about historical helmets where streets are narrow, there's difficult access and there's no place for shelters and complications on all sides; if, moreover, we have to do archaeological excavations — in case of Lugo, I say it again, but there are archaeological excavations in the Coruña and in other nuclei alike -- that is, we're talking about a very costly process not only in time — that goes on for years and years — but economically for the owners of these buildings. It is therefore very difficult to carry out these rehabilitations, to bore them before they begin, and we have an obligation, as Members and Members responsible for legislating, to draw up a rule that facilitates — of course, respecting the heritage — these interventions. That is why there is a need for economic support for administrations in this case too, taking all these situations into account. I'm referring to the situation of historical helmets, whether excavations are necessary, etc., etc., because all of these expenses are currently borne by the rehabilitation agencies or persons. And if those archaeological remains are public, I think you should participate and collaborate in some way, look for ways to support these excavations. We must bear in mind that the bet on rehabilitation is a clear fact and it is a bet on which the Administration will always win every time it bets on it — and I don't like bets, I want it to be clear — but we must bear in mind that for every million euros that an administration puts in aid to individuals for rehabilitation this usually leads to an investment that is multiplied by at least three. I mean, we'd be talking, with 1 million, with a minimum investment of about 3 million euros, that we'd be generating around 200 jobs; we'd be generating an impact again on the different administrations — councils, state administration and also autonomy — because practically around the same million euros that you're contributing. Because we don't forget that there are licenses, social security, IVE, IBI, etc., and even the income of the workers who are going to have a salary are working there. So I think it's a money that would be very well invested, worth it, and there we must work together. Of course, the aim of this initiative is that all groups made contributions. I'll talk after the various amendments. But if I wish, before time runs out, to thank the understanding of the spirit of this initiative by Ms. Maria Luisa Pierres, of the Socialist Parliamentary Group, who makes important contributions, that I am convinced that many of them are already planned and contemplated in the draft of the law that the Government has, but not that is why they are less important; that is to say, of course invite her to follow that line of participation and bring out issues to solve the problems and really give solutions to rehabilitation in our country. Thank you all very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-11-07-DSPG046.u210	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Once again, good afternoon. I don't know why every crisis, rather than reflect, the People's Party overthrows and gives them budget for the media — EUR 77,000 — as a measure of concealment. The means of extinction — Mr President said this morning — the most varicose-looking people in the world carry one third of the budget of the Rural Council. Ordering is chaos and the fires of October 15th, many of them interfaces, they tied feet and hands the troops. If the countryside was not abandoned, if the mountain was a socially assumed and starred space, if the multifunctionality replaced the robbery installed in the monoculture, we would be discussing aspects and not essences at this time. Well, let's go to the negated fires. Until the '90s and '90s, most fires, still with various complications, were controlled by four fundamental reasons. One. Because the dispersed population entities did not have to protect them, because they were already for agricultural activity; much of the slaughterhouse fuel was used as fertilizer and as a bed for cattle; around the inhabited nuclei there were lands of cultivation and there was no social acceptance of the proximity of dense masses of mountain, treed or not, near the dwellings. Therefore, the activity of the fire prevention and extinction service was more offensive than defensive. Two. The forest masses did not have the current volume or were so large; the meadows separated them and cut them off; today, after the abandonment of agricultural activity and reduced dairy farms to one tenth, Mr. Dani Vega, to one tenth. It achieves the desertification of the countryside, the occupation by the trees of thousands of hectares of land land makes the fire of October 15 truly not reach the Cantabrian in all its splendor. Three. The new climate cycle, with higher temperatures, relative humiditys -- which has already been mentioned to satiety here -- were unknown in our country, because they didn't provoke those spread speeds that scare us so much at the moment. Four. The atomization of the fire extinguishing service, under the responsibility of the Union, with the continuing weight loss in this, in particular, seriously affects the effectiveness. In the fire of Verín this year, the entire air power of Galicia was dissipated. In what happened in 2005, there were many simultaneous fires and there was much less water power; and yet this time the fire advanced faster than that. The fourth-generation fires -- the so-called interfaces -- generate multi-emergency with impacts both on housing and people and, of course, on average, can generate situations of trapping at risk for human lives. Of course, the priority is the protection of citizenship. The fireman acts in front of the fire to defend himself. If we add to this a propagation rate that in some specific cases can reach speeds of 40 meters/minute, situations can become uncontrollable. The fire goes in front of the firemen, the fire runs in front of the fire, or behind the fire. So, means of protecting citizenship are diverted without the appropriate protocols to combine with other emergency services; it's chaos; there's no clear protocol that says how far the forest fireman is, up to where the urban fireman, up to where Civil Protection, Police, Civil Guard... The fires that were up to ninety in Galicia had an average propagation speed of about 10 meters/minute, an hour advanced 600 meters, a means of normal air and land extinction was able to contain it and dominate it. Leiria's fire, in Portugal, advanced 55 meters/minutes, i.e., in an hour three and a half kilometers, in all directions, convective fire. There's no means to cut this fire. A convective fire is what moves secondary lights in all directions at great distances; therefore, it needs no wind to propagate, create its own winds and back. The propagation speed of the Verín fire this summer was around 28 meters/minute, so it was 18 meters/minute above the controller by a normal brigade and with normal means; it was a fire out of the capacity for extinction. So if the media start working on the flank, because there's a lot of smoke on the front, they never get it. It all indicates that there is a need for serious reflection right now, with the participation of all the elements that work in the prevention and extinction service, what to do and how to deal with the challenges of interface and fifth-generation fires. To the dramatic warning that the fire of Leiria has given us, we must add our own experience of October 15th. The conditions were extreme — 30-30-30 — but they were also extreme to the incompetence and improvisation of the People's Party's Government. Looks like we all knew it could happen, all except the government. Can you... It can happen again at any time, the fuel is ready. The failure to comply with the law on home security lines and infrastructure makes the threat there, always present. It's certainly the most urgent problem we have to solve. No one understands that after several serious threats, the Government of the People's Party has not taken measures and launched balloons outside. All of this should make us think deeply about these fires and analyze them in time, designing strategies that avoid improvisation and ineffectiveness. No one here sat on a table to analyze how a convective fire works, like Leiria fire, how an interface fire works, how it combines with the Civil Guard, urban firefighters, etc. All this, in our view, should at this point be contrasted, studied and protocoled. We have professionals of extinction with knowledge and experience enough to shape and prepare the service for these continences. Reflection, protocol and formation would be the steps to take; form, homogenize, stabilize, unify and put an end to so much precariousness and heterogeneousness in the service; translated into common: chiringuits. That's another song. For all of the above, this Parliamentary Group of the BNG urges the Government of Greece to create a body — and here we will admit the amendment made to us by the Socialist Party — that counts with the active participation of the various collectives who form part of the entire apparatus that intervenes in the device of prevention and extinction, to jointly analyse the four-and-a-fifth-generation fires that exist, they are there. The other day they showed us that our firefighters, the brigades, the people... everyone was defending the houses, and the fire was belling through their broads where they weren't houses, and where there were houses too, unfortunately. This is a reality we cannot ignore. Hiding under the wing is a matter of ostriches and other animals; rational human beings don't, and we shouldn't. So we urge the Galician Union to observe these fires, to put on the batteries, to change the batteries, to load the batteries and to start taking ourselves seriously; not the forest, the mountain, because the mountain is a whole, the forest is a part. Thank you very much.

Secondly, to initiate the procedure for the declaration as an asset of cultural interest of the previous sculptures and, above all, to act to ensure that the declaration has the legal effects laid down by the Heritage Law. And thirdly, to proceed to the cataloguing of all the goods existing within the palace of Meirás in order to ensure their legal protection, because, as we say, the history of the sculpture spolium is part of this long history of spy spolium which continues to have consequences to this day. Nothing else.
